15.1.2. Data and Safety Monitoring Committee,
Trial 3001
An independent data monitoring committee (IDMC) was established to monitor the safety and
efficacy data generated during the trial. The IDMC was comprised of an independent, unblinded
statistician and independent clinical specialists in the fields of HAE management who have
experience in clinical trials. The IDMC reviewed accumulating data during the study in an
unblinded manner to advise on the further conduct of the study. No success or futility thresholds
were established for IDMC reviews (the Applicant would not stop the study unless a major
safety issue was identified). No safety concerns were identified during the IDMC meetings, and
the IDMC recommended that the study continue as planned.

15.1.3. Sample Size Considerations, Trial 3001
The Applicant determined that 40 subjects (allocated 3:2 to garadacimab: placebo) with C1-INH
(Type I or Type II) HAE completing the 6-month Treatment Period would be needed to achieve
a power of approximately 90% via a two-sided Wilcoxon test at alpha=5%. Monthly HAE attack
rates of 0.3125 and 1.3 were assumed for the garadacimab and placebo arms (equivalent to a
76% reduction relative to placebo) and were assumed to be Poisson distributed (the assumed
monthly attack rates were used as the two means of the Poisson distributions).
The assumed attack rate for the garadacimab arm was based on the results of Treatment Period 1
in the phase 2 trial (2001; see Section 16.1), where the mean of the individual attack rates for the
24 subjects who were administered 75 mg, 200 mg, or 600 mg garadacimab was calculated as
0.3125 attacks per month. To be conservative, the mean attack rate of the three pooled doses was
used instead of the mean attack rate for only the 200 mg garadacimab arm, as the 200 mg
garadacimab arm showed the highest treatment effect among the three doses. The assumed attack
rate for the placebo arm was based on a publication of a prior phase 3 study of lanadelumab (not
conducted by the Applicant) for the prevention of HAE attacks (Banerji et al. 2018), where an
attack rate of 0.3 attacks per week was assumed (which is equivalent to 1.3 attacks per month).
The Applicant performed the sample size calculation for this study using the “proc power”
procedure in SAS (version 9.4), which uses the O’Brien-Castelloe approach to compute the
approximate power. The randomization ratio of 3:2 was implemented by using “GROUPNS=(24
16)”, resulting in a total sample size of 40, which was the lowest one reaching a power of 90%.
To allow for sufficient safety data, to increase the likelihood of adolescents entering the study,
and to have at least 40 subjects reaching the end of the study, an additional 20 subjects were
planned to be randomized (for a total planned sample size of about 60 subjects). The Applicant
also aimed to randomize approximately 5 adolescent subjects and approximately 5 Japanese
subjects among the 60 total subjects.
The study included a total of 64 subjects (39 on garadacimab and 25 on placebo) and met the
Applicant’s main enrollment goal (though one subject on placebo was excluded from efficacy
analyses due to receiving less than 30 days of study treatment). The Applicant also met their
other enrollment goals (6 adolescent subjects and 6 Japanese subjects were included in the
study).

222
Integrated Review Template, version 3.0 (05/25/2022)
Reference ID: 5458438

BLA 761367
Andembry / garadacimab
The sample size for this phase 3 trial was comparable to prior similar HAE programs and was
generally reasonable, as HAE is a rare condition.

15.1.4. Changes to the Planned Analyses, Trial 3001
The Applicant made the following changes prior to database lock in relation to the prespecified
analyses in the original clinical study protocol (dated August 4, 2020):
1. Estimand definitions were updated to reflect the potential impact of the COVID-19 pandemic
No subjects discontinued treatment or the study as a result of the COVID-19 pandemic.
1. Comparison of the distribution of responses to therapy (via the Subject’s Global Assessment
of Response to Therapy (SGART)) between the garadacimab and placebo arms at the end of
the 6-month Treatment Period used data at Visit Day 182 only. A two-sided chi-squared test
at alpha=5% was used. If a subject’s SGART value at Visit Day 182 was missing, then the
subject was not included in the exploratory analysis.
2. Definitions for H02 and H04 were modified.
3. For the primary estimand, the intercurrent event of early discontinuation due or not due to
COVID-19 was removed.
The Applicant removed this intercurrent event per FDA feedback, as the Agency did not
consider this to be an intercurrent event.
1. Exploratory analyses of patient-reported outcome endpoints (the angioedema quality of life,
EQ-5D-5L, Work Productivity and Activity Impairment, and Investigator’s Global
Assessment of Response to Therapy) were conducted in the intent-to-treat (ITT) Analysis Set
rather than the Safety Analysis Set.
No changes were made to the Applicant’s prespecified analyses after database lock. However,
the following additional analyses were conducted after database lock:
1. The time-normalized number of HAE attacks within 3 months before Screening for subjects
with and without HAE prophylaxis during 3 months before Screening (for HAE history using
the ITT Analysis Set).
2. The time-normalized number of HAE attacks during the 3 months before Screening or start
of HAE prophylaxis (for HAE history using the ITT Analysis Set).
3. The time-normalized number of HAE attacks per month for the first and second 3 months of
treatment in the ITT Analysis Set using a generalized linear model for count data assuming a
Poisson distribution.
4. The time-normalized number of HAE attacks per month requiring on-demand therapy in the
ITT Analysis Set using a Poisson modeled structured similarly to the Applicant’s sensitivity
analysis for the primary endpoint described in Section 6.2.3.2, where the baseline attack rate
only included HAE attacks that required on-demand therapy during the Run-in Period.
5. The time-normalized number of moderate or severe HAE attacks per month in the ITT
Analysis Set using a Poisson modeled structured similarly to the Applicant’s sensitivity
analysis for the primary endpoint described in Section 6.2.3.2, where the baseline attack rate
only included HAE attacks that were moderate or severe during the Run-in Period.
The results of analyses #4 and #5 above were submitted by the Applicant at the Agency’s request
during the review cycle. The results of these analyses can be found in Section 6.2.4.5.

15.1.5. Protocol Amendments, Trial 3001
The original clinical study protocol (dated August 4, 2020) had no substantial amendments and
had one administrative amendment (dated December 8, 2020) that only added clarification of an
exclusion criterion related to use of contraception. A Japanese version of the protocol (dated
January 15, 2021) was created to include study sites in Japan, which contained several minor
administrative adjustments required for compliance with local regulations in Japan.
None of the above changes substantially impacted the study design or results.

15.1.6. Safety Assessments, Trial 3001
Safety assessments included AE reporting, laboratory parameters (clinical chemistry,
hematology, coagulation, urinalysis), vital signs, physical examinations, and EKG testing
according to the Schedules of Assessments (see Figure 60 and Figure 61). Labs were collected at
baseline and Months 3 and 6. Subjects had an additional lab collection on Day 1 if baseline labs
were collected more than 90 days prior to the first dose of garadacimab administration.
Pregnancy tests, urinalyses, and hematology, chemistry, and coagulation labs were processed at a
central laboratory. Overall, the safety monitoring in trial 3001 was adequate to inform the safety
profile of garadacimab for the proposed use.

224
Integrated Review Template, version 3.0 (05/25/2022)
Reference ID: 5458438

BLA 761367
Andembry / garadacimab
After Screening, eligible subjects entered the Run-in Period lasting at least 1 month and up to
2 months to confirm underlying disease status and to assess eligibility for participation in the
Treatment Period. The first day of the Run-in Period could have occurred on the same day as
Screening. Subjects had to experience ≥2 HAE attacks during the Run-in Period to be eligible to
enter the Treatment Period. Subjects who experienced ≥2 HAE attacks during the required first
month of the Run-in Period could immediately enter the Treatment Period. Subjects who did not
experience an HAE attack during the first month of the Run-in Period remained in the
Run-in Period for up to 2 months, during which time they were required to experience ≥2 HAE
attacks to be eligible to enter the Treatment Period. If a subject was unable to enter the
Treatment Period after Day 60, then approval from the Applicant was required for the subject to
extend the Run-in Period for another month to enter the Treatment Period within 3 months.
Subjects were prohibited from using routine prophylaxis to prevent HAE attacks during the
Run-in Period; however, subjects could have used on-demand HAE therapy (i.e., C1-INH
replacement or icatibant or ecallantide) to treat HAE attacks if the medication was previously
shown to be effective. Subjects who did not meet the minimum HAE attack rate during the
Run-in Period or were otherwise determined to be ineligible due to Screening assessments were
considered Run-in failures and were not rescreened.
Subjects meeting all eligibility criteria entered the Treatment Period after the Run-in Period. If a
subject was unable to enter the Treatment Period after Day 60, then approval from the Applicant
was required for the subject to enter the Treatment Period. During the Treatment Period, subjects
were assigned to 200 mg garadacimab administered SC once per month. For garadacimab-naïve
subjects, a loading dose of 400 mg garadacimab (two 200 mg doses) was administered SC on
Visit Day 1, then 200 mg per month in subsequent months. The Treatment Period had a variable
duration for individual subjects; however, each subject received garadacimab for at least
12 months.
For the Follow-up Period, subjects were contacted approximately two months after their EoT
Visit to complete final study assessments.
For details regarding the collection and reporting of HAE data, and the investigator confirmation
of HAE attacks, see Section 15.1.1.
15.2.1.1. Sample Size Assumptions, Study 3002
Approximately 150 subjects (including 8 Japanese subjects) were planned to be enrolled in the
study, with a minimum of 100 subjects having at least 12 months of exposure to garadacimab.
Since this was an open label, single-arm study with no control arm, the sample size for this study
was not based on a formal calculation but on the International Council for Harmonisation
guidance, E1A The Extent of Population Exposure to Assess Clinical Safety for Drugs Intended
for Long-Term Treatment of Non-Life-Threatening Conditions (October 1995). The Applicant
claimed that a sample size of 100 subjects would allow for the observation of ≥1 AE with a
probability of 3% at a 95% confidence level.

month. Study visits, vitals, physical examinations, laboratory monitoring, and AE monitoring for

15.2.4. Analysis Populations, Study 3002
The following analysis sets were defined by the Applicant in the final statistical analysis plan
(statistical analysis plan (SAP); version 1.0; dated June 25, 2021):

up period. Screening and Run-in periods were not performed for rollover subjects (instead,
relevant endpoint data, such as HAE attacks during the Run-in Period, were used from
trials 2001 and 3001). The durations of the Treatment/Efficacy and Safety evaluation periods (in
days) were calculated as: End Date − Start Date + 1 Day. The durations of the Screening, Run-in,

15.2.6. Primary Analysis Description, Study 3002
The primary endpoint of TEAEs was provided as number of subjects, percentage of subjects, and
number of events. The TEAE rates per injection and per subject-year were also reported.

15.2.7. Secondary Efficacy Analysis Description,
Study 3002
The secondary efficacy endpoints were analyzed using the ATS Analysis Set. The timenormalized number of HAE attacks was also analyzed using the Per Protocol Analysis Set.
For all analyses considering HAE attacks, only investigator-confirmed attacks were used.
Subjects entered HAE symptoms into an eDiary (with the start and end date and time), along
with location of HAE symptoms, use of on-demand therapy to treat HAE symptoms, and
interference of HAE symptoms with daily activities. The investigator confirmed HAE attacks
based on a review of the eDiary, relevant medical history, and physician judgment.
For garadacimab-naïve subjects, HAE attacks with a start date on or after the first day of the
Run-in Period but prior to the date and time of the first study drug administration were counted
for the Run-in Period. For subjects from the phase 2 (2001) or phase 3 (3001) trial, the number of
HAE attacks during the Run-in Period of the previous study was used. HAE attacks with a start
date and time after the first study drug administration through the EoT Visit were counted for the
Treatment Period and were included in the efficacy analyses.
The time-normalized number of HAE attacks per month were calculated per subject as:
[number of HAE attacks ÷ length of subject treatment in days] × 30.4375
Length of subject treatment in days was calculated as the duration of the Treatment/Efficacy
evaluation period defined in Table 110.
The time-normalized number of HAE attacks per month were summarized descriptively using
the mean and median with corresponding 95% CIs.
Reduction in HAE attack rate during the Treatment Period compared to the Run-in Period was
summarized descriptively as a percent reduction per subject, calculated as:
100% × (1 − [time-normalized number of HAE attacks per month during
treatment ÷ time-normalized number of HAE attacks per month
during the Run-in Period]).
The number and percentage of responders and nonresponders were also presented with
corresponding 95% CIs. A subject was classified as a responder if their percent reduction in
HAE attacks during treatment compared to the Run-in Period was ≥50%. The number and
percentage of subjects with reductions of ≥70%, ≥90%, and 100% (i.e., attack-free during
236
Integrated Review Template, version 3.0 (05/25/2022)
Reference ID: 5458438

BLA 761367
Andembry / garadacimab
treatment) were also reported with associated 95% CIs. Exact Clopper-Pearson CIs were used for
all percentages.
The time-normalized number of HAE attacks per month requiring on-demand therapy was
summarized descriptively, calculated per subject as:
[number of HAE attacks requiring on-demand therapy ÷
length of subject treatment in days] × 30.4375.
Length of subject treatment in days was calculated as the duration of the Treatment/Efficacy
evaluation period defined in Table 110.
The time-normalized number of moderate and/or severe HAE attacks per month was calculated
and analyzed similarly to the total time-normalized number of HAE attacks per month (described
above).
Finally, SGART results were presented descriptively by study visit where assessed. The number
and percentage of subjects with each rating (“none”, “poor”, “fair”, “good”, or “excellent”) were
reported, along with cumulative responses (“poor or better”, “fair or better”, and “good or
excellent”). A two-sided 95% CI was reported for the percentages for each cumulative response
category. An exploratory Friedman test at alpha=5% was conducted to compare the percentage
of subjects with responses of “good” or “excellent” between Visit Day 361 and Visit Day 721.

15.2.8. Sensitivity and Supportive Statistical Analyses
Description, Study 3002
To evaluate the impact of the COVID-19 pandemic on the primary endpoint (safety), the
overview of TEAEs was repeated as a sensitivity analysis excluding all TEAEs related to
COVID-19. As a supportive analysis, a summary table of the TEAEs associated with COVID-19
was reported for subjects with C1-INH (Type I or Type II) HAE.
As supplementary analyses, all secondary endpoints (efficacy), except SGART, were assessed
over time in 3-month (91-day) time windows with the first window starting on the Month 1,
Day 1 Visit. The time-normalized number of HAE attacks and the time-normalized number of
HAE attacks requiring on-demand therapy were also assessed from Visit Day 1 until Visit
Day 31 of the Treatment Period. This was repeated for moderate and severe HAE attacks.

15.2.9. Changes to the Planned Analyses, Study 3002
The Applicant did not report any major changes to the analyses described in the final statistical
analysis plan (SAP; version 1.0; dated June 25, 2021).
For the secondary safety endpoints, the Applicant planned to present tables for the C1-INH
(Type I or Type II) HAE subjects, the non-C1-INH HAE subjects, and overall. Instead, only the
summary of the TEAEs were presented for the three groups above, and all other secondary safety
endpoints were presented for the overall study population only.

15.2.10. Interim Analyses, Study 3002
An initial interim analysis was planned to be conducted when approximately 100 subjects had
12 months of exposure to garadacimab in study 3002 alone or across all three studies (i.e.,
trials 2001 and 3001 and study 3002).
Results from a second interim analysis, completed after approximately 119 subjects had at least
12 months of exposure to garadacimab in study 3002 alone or across all three studies, are
presented in the Applicant’s Interim Clinical Study Report (dated August 10, 2023). The data
cutoff dates for the second interim analysis were February 13, 2023 (for all subjects except
adolescents) and May 22, 2023 (for adolescents).
Additional interim analyses were conducted on an as-needed basis to support regulatory
activities. The results of the interim analyses were not intended to be used for stopping or
adapting the study or adjusting the sample size.

15.2.11. Data and Safety Monitoring Committee,
Study 3002
An IDMC was established to monitor the safety and efficacy data generated during the study.
The IDMC reviewed accumulating data from the ongoing study. No success or futility thresholds
were set for IDMC reviews. The Applicant planned to continue the trial unless a safety issue that
warranted study termination was confirmed. The composition, activities, analysis,
responsibilities, and timing of meetings of the IDMC were outlined in the IDMC Charter.

Reference ID: 5458438

BLA 761367
Andembry / garadacimab
Subjects who provided written informed consent were screened for their eligibility to enter the
Run-in Period. Screening was completed within 28 days before the first day of the
Run-in Period.
After Screening, eligible subjects entered the Run-in Period, which lasted a minimum of 4 weeks
and up to 8 weeks, to confirm their underlying disease status and to assess their eligibility for
participation in Treatment Period 1. Subjects were not permitted to use routine prophylaxis to
prevent HAE attacks during the Run-in Period, but subjects could use on-demand HAE therapy
(i.e., C1-INH replacement or icatibant or ecallantide) to treat emerging HAE attacks. Subjects
completed the Run-in Period and could begin Treatment Period 1 when the following criteria
were met:


Subjects with C1-INH (Type I or Type II) HAE must have experienced ≥2 HAE attacks
within a consecutive 4-week period during the Run-in Period.



Subjects with FXII/PLG (Type III) HAE must have experienced ≥1 HAE attack during the
Run-in Period.

Subjects entered Treatment Period 1 no later than 14 days after the Week 9 (Day 57) telephone
contact during the Run-in Period. If this was not possible for the subject, then approval was
required from the Applicant.
In Treatment Period 1, 32 subjects were randomized 1:1:1:1 to receive 75 mg, 200 mg, or
600 mg garadacimab SC every 4 weeks for 13 weeks, or matched placebo. Additional subjects
(N=6) were assigned to 400 mg garadacimab SC Q2W for 13 weeks (open-label). Subjects
documented HAE attack onset, symptoms, and use of on-demand medications in an eDiary.
During each visit, the Investigator reviewed the eDiary and documented HAE attacks.
Outside of the planned randomization algorithm, six subjects with Type III HAE were enrolled.
These six subjects consisted of three subjects with FXII mutation and three with PLG mutation.
Once the FXII/PLG (Type III) HAE subjects satisfied all Run-in Period criteria, they were
directly assigned to receive 600 mg garadacimab SC every 4 weeks (open-label).
All subjects who participated in Treatment Period 1 were able to participate in
Treatment Period 2, which lasted 44 weeks and was extended up until another garadacimab study
was opened for subjects to join or until the current study was discontinued. In
Treatment Period 2 (open-label), subjects either continued on their regimen (if they were already
assigned to 200 mg or 600 mg garadacimab SC every 4 weeks in Treatment Period 1) or were
moved to the 200 mg and 600 mg garadacimab arms. After the first planned interim analysis, it
was determined that there was no difference in efficacy between the 200 mg and 600 mg
garadacimab arms, but there were significantly more injection site reactions in the 600 mg
garadacimab arm. Therefore, a protocol amendment in March 2020 reassigned all Type I and
Type II HAE subjects to 200 mg garadacimab every 4 weeks. Subjects with Type III HAE
continued on 600 mg garadacimab every 4 weeks.
The overall design was generally reasonable for a proof of concept, dose-ranging trial in a
development program for an HAE prophylactic therapy.

schedules of assessments are acceptable for a phase 2, dose-ranging trial.

15.3.3. Eligibility Criteria, Trial 2001
Key inclusion and exclusion criteria for this trial are summarized below. These criteria appear
reasonable for a phase 2 HAE trial.
Key Inclusion Criteria


Adults 18 to 65 years of age.



A diagnosis of C1-INH (Type I or Type II) HAE or FXII/PLG (Type III) HAE as
demonstrated by clinical history consistent with HAE (SC or mucosal, nonpruritic swelling
episodes without accompanying urticaria) and supportive laboratory values:
– C1-INH antigen concentration or function activity <50% of the lower limit of the
reference range and C4 antigen concentration below the lower limit of the reference
range (for subjects with Type I or Type II HAE).
– HAE-associated FXII or PLG gene mutation (for subjects with Type III HAE).



For baseline HAE attack rate:
– Four or more HAE attacks over a consecutive 2-month period during the 3 months before
Screening, as documented in the subject’s medical record (for subjects with Type I or
Type II HAE).
– One or more HAE attack during the 3 months before screening, as documented in the
subject’s medical record (for subjects with Type III HAE).
– For subjects on prophylactic HAE therapy, documentation of baseline HAE attack rate
prior to prophylactic HAE therapy was accepted.



Willing to cease the use of C1-INH products, androgens, or antifibrinolytics for routine
prophylaxis against HAE attacks on the first day of the Run-in Period, after being assessed
by the investigator to be able to adequately manage on-demand treatments of HAE attacks
without assistance.

Key Exclusion Criteria


History of clinically significant arterial or venous thrombosis, or current clinically significant
prothrombotic risk (including presence of a central venous access device).



History of uncontrolled, abnormal bleeding events due to a coagulopathy, or a current
clinically significant coagulopathy, or clinically significant risks for bleeding events.



Had any preplanned surgeries during the trial that had an inherent clinically significant risk
for thrombotic events or bleeding.



Had an incurable malignancy.



For subjects with Type I or Type II HAE, a clinically significant history of poor response to
C1-INH therapy for the management of HAE.



Female subjects with Type I or Type II HAE who initiated or changed dose of any hormonal
contraceptive regimen or hormone replacement therapy (i.e., estrogen/progesterone products)
within 3 months before Screening.
246

Integrated Review Template, version 3.0 (05/25/2022)
Reference ID: 5458438

BLA 761367
Andembry / garadacimab


Known hypersensitivity to the investigational product or any of its excipients.

15.3.4. Primary and Secondary Objectives and
Endpoints, Trial 2001
Primary Objectives and Endpoints
The primary objective of this study was to evaluate the dose and efficacy of garadacimab in the
prevention of HAE attacks in subjects with C1-INH (Type I or Type II) HAE. The primary
endpoint was the time-normalized number of investigator-confirmed HAE attacks per month
during the blinded, placebo-controlled portion of the study (Treatment Period 1). The primary
endpoint was calculated per subject as:
[number of HAE attacks ÷ length of subject’s evaluation period in days] × 30.4375.
A subject’s evaluation period depended on whether the subject received an IV loading dose. All
32 subjects with Type I or Type II HAE who were assigned to 75 mg, 200 mg, or 600 mg
garadacimab, or matched placebo, in Treatment Period 1 received an IV loading dose. For these
subjects, their evaluation periods were from Day 6, or the date of first SC administration of the
investigational product, until Day 91 or the Study Completion Visit date (whichever occurred
first).
The additional six subjects who were assigned to 400 mg garadacimab Q2W (open-label) in
Treatment Period 1 did not receive an IV loading dose. For these subjects, their evaluation
periods were from Day 15, or the date of second SC administration of the investigational
product, until Day 85 or the Study Completion Visit date (whichever occurred first).
Secondary Objectives and Endpoints
The secondary objectives of this study included evaluation of the efficacy of garadacimab in
subjects with C1-INH (Type I or Type II) HAE. The secondary efficacy endpoints included:


Number and percentage of subjects with Type I or Type II HAE who had a ≥50% reduction
in the time-normalized number of HAE attacks per month during Treatment Period 1
compared to the Run-in Period.



Number and percentage of subjects with Type I or Type II HAE who were attack-free during
Treatment Period 1.



Number, time-normalized number (per month), and percentage of mild, moderate, and severe
HAE attacks in subjects with Type I or Type II HAE during Treatment Period 1.



Number, time-normalized number (per month), and percentage of mild, moderate, and severe
HAE attacks treated with on-demand therapy in subjects with Type I or Type II HAE during
Treatment Period 1.

15.3.5. Statistical Analysis Plan, Trial 2001
The final statistical analysis plan (SAP; version 2.0) is dated June 19, 2020. The analysis sets
defined by the Applicant for this study included the following:
247
Integrated Review Template, version 3.0 (05/25/2022)
Reference ID: 5458438

BLA 761367
Andembry / garadacimab
Screening Population: All subjects who provided written informed consent and underwent any
study screening procedure.
ITT Population: All subjects who provided informed consent, underwent any study screening
procedure, and was assigned to treatment in Treatment Period 1 or to treatment in
Treatment Period 2, regardless of whether they received the investigational product. The ITT
Analysis Set was analyzed using the treatment to which the subject was assigned in
Treatment Period 1, in Treatment Period 2 at Week 13, or 200 mg after dose reduction from
600 mg following Amendment 2 in Treatment Period 2 (only applicable for C1-INH [Type I or
Type II] HAE subjects), regardless of the actual treatment received. This was the main set used
for efficacy analyses. Subjects with FXII/PLG (Type III) HAE were not included.
Safety Population: All subjects who provided informed consent, were assigned to treatment in
Treatment Period 1 or to treatment in Treatment Period 2 and received at least 1 dose or partial
dose of the investigational product. The Safety Population was based on the actual treatment
received and was the main set used for safety analyses.
Per Protocol Population: All subjects in the ITT Analysis Set, excluding subjects who had a
significant protocol violation. Protocol violations leading to exclusion from the Per Protocol
Population were reviewed and agreed upon prior to conducting each of the interim analyses and
before database lock.

15.3.6. Analysis of the Primary Endpoint, Trial 2001
The primary analysis tested for a difference in the time-normalized number of HAE attacks per
month during Treatment Period 1 between 200 mg garadacimab versus placebo and between
600 mg garadacimab versus placebo at a full alpha level of 5%. A two-sided Mann-Whitney test
was used for both comparisons (each at alpha=2.5% to account for multiple testing). An
exploratory Mann-Whitney test was conducted to compare 200 mg garadacimab and 600 mg
garadacimab at alpha=5%. No testing was done for the 75 mg and 400 mg garadacimab arms.
Handling of Missing Data
While strategies for addressing missing data were prespecified in the final SAP, no missing data
occurred for the primary endpoint. Therefore, no missing data or sensitivity analyses were
conducted by the Applicant.
Subgroup Analyses
Subgroup analyses for the primary endpoint were not prespecified in the protocol or final SAP or
submitted by the Applicant.

15.3.7. Analysis of the Secondary Endpoint, Trial 2001
All secondary endpoints in Section 15.3.3 were investigated exploratorily via descriptive
statistics. No statistical testing was conducted for these endpoints.

15.3.8. Data and Safety Monitoring Committee,
Trial 2001
An IDMC was established to monitor safety, efficacy, and PK/PD data generated during the trial.
The IDMC was comprised of independent clinical specialists in HAE with experience in clinical
trials. The IDMC reviewed accumulating data from the ongoing study in an unblinded manner.
No safety concerns were identified during the IDMC meetings, and the IDMC recommended that
the study continue as planned.

15.3.9. Sample Size Considerations, Trial 2001
The Applicant determined that seven subjects with C1-INH (Type I or Type II) HAE assigned to
each tested treatment arm (200 mg garadacimab, 600 mg garadacimab, or placebo) were needed
to reach about 82% power for the comparisons of 200 mg garadacimab versus placebo and
600 mg garadacimab versus placebo in Treatment Period 1, under the following assumptions:


Alpha=2.5% for each comparison using two-sided Mann-Whitney tests.



The time-normalized number of HAE attacks per month was 2.9 in the placebo arm (derived
from a previous HAE trial [3001] conducted by the Applicant, where the lower bound of the
distribution-free 95% CI for the median in the first placebo Treatment Period was used).



The time-normalized number of HAE attacks per month was 0.6 in the garadacimab arms
(derived from a previous HAE trial [3001] conducted by the Applicant, where the upper
bound of the distribution-free 95% CI for the median of the combined active Treatment
Periods was used).



The time-normalized HAE attack rates follow a Poisson distribution, where the lower and
upper bounds of the 95% CIs for the medians are used as the rate parameters.



Randomization allocation is equal.

The 75 mg garadacimab arm was included for PK/PD reasons and was not expected to yield
maximum clinical efficacy. The sample size of seven subjects with Type I or Type II HAE for
this arm was not driven by an efficacy comparison or power calculation, but to maintain study
blinding and consistency with the other treatment arms. No testing for efficacy was planned for
the 75 mg garadacimab arm versus placebo.
An additional 1 subject with Type I or Type II HAE was planned to be added to each of the four
treatment arms above to account for dropouts during Treatment Period 1, resulting in eight
subjects with Type I or Type II HAE planned per treatment arm. A total of 32 subjects with
Type I or Type II HAE were randomized to the blinded, placebo-controlled portion of the study
(Treatment Period 1).
An additional eight subjects with Type I or Type II HAE were planned to be assigned to 400 mg
garadacimab Q2W (open-label) and an additional 10 subjects with FXII/PLG (Type III) HAE
were planned to be assigned to 600 mg garadacimab every 4 weeks (open-label). A power
calculation was not performed for these two treatment arms; they were included for
informational purposes only and were not planned to be tested against placebo. In the study,
249
Integrated Review Template, version 3.0 (05/25/2022)
Reference ID: 5458438

BLA 761367
Andembry / garadacimab
there were six subjects with Type I or Type II HAE in the 400 mg garadacimab arm (open-label)
and six subjects with Type III HAE in the 600 mg garadacimab arm (open-label).
Overall, a total of up to 40 subjects with Type I or Type II HAE were planned to be assigned to
treatment in Treatment Period 1. In the study, a total of 38 subjects with Type I or Type II HAE
were enrolled.
Treatment Period 2 was open-label and comprised subjects from Treatment Period 1. Therefore,
no sample size calculations were performed for Treatment Period 2.

15.3.10. Protocol Amendments, Trial 2001
The original protocol (dated May 30, 2018) had two substantial amendments: Amendment 1
(dated December 18, 2018) and Amendment 2 (dated March 20, 2020).
Amendment 1 included updates to pregnancy testing, acceptable methods of contraception and
use of routine (long-term) HAE prophylaxis, and the duration of the Follow-up Period (extended
to 85 days), and clarifications were made to the statistical methods (before database lock).
In Amendment 2, all subjects with C1-INH (Type I or Type II) HAE receiving 600 mg
garadacimab in Treatment Period 2 had their doses decreased to 200 mg, and corresponding edits
were made to the Statistical Analyses and Methods section of the protocol (before database
lock).
Additionally, Contingency Amendment 1 (dated April 9, 2020) was produced in response to the
COVID-19 pandemic, which allowed all subjects in Treatment Period 2 to substitute a research
center visit with a remote telephone or video conference.

15.3.11. Changes to the Planned Analyses, Trial 2001
The original SAP (SAP; version 1.0; dated April 1, 2020) was modified to include updates to
align with Amendment 2 and Contingency Amendment 1 of the original protocol (described
above). All changes are reflected in the final SAP (version 2.0; dated June 19, 2020) and were
generally reasonable.

16. Efficacy
16.1. Durability of Treatment Effect, Study 3002
A total of 161 subjects (151 adults and 10 adolescents) were enrolled in an open-label extension
study (3002), of which 90 (56%) were garadacimab-naïve (69 new subjects and 21 subjects from
trial 3001) and 71 (44%) were previously treated with garadacimab (35 subjects from trial 2001
and 36 subjects from trial 3001). Of the 161 subjects, 145 (90%) had Type I HAE, 14 (9%) had
Type II HAE, and 2 (1%) had Type III HAE. The mean duration of garadacimab treatment was
14 months (range: 3 to 21 months), with 119 subjects (74%) having received garadacimab for
≥12 months. As shown in Table 115, the mean time-normalized number of HAE attacks per
month was 0.16 (95% CI=0.1, 0.22), which is generally consistent with the primary analysis
250
Integrated Review Template, version 3.0 (05/25/2022)
Reference ID: 5458438

BLA 761367
Andembry / garadacimab
results from the pivotal study (Table 10). This treatment effect was sustained in 55 subjects
(34%) who received garadacimab for >15 months (mean=0.15; 95% CI=0.05, 0.25). However,
because of the lack of a control arm and blinding, these results should be interpreted with
caution.

16.2. Results of Statistical Analyses, Trial 2001
This section summarizes subject disposition, baseline demographics and clinical characteristics,
and the primary and secondary efficacy results.
Subject Disposition
Disposition information is provided in Figure 66. A total of 54 subjects were screened, of which
2 were screen failures due to not having a clinical diagnosis of C1-INH (Type I or Type II) HAE
and reasons related to use of contraception or hormone replacement therapy. Of the 52 subjects
who entered the Run-in Period, 14 failed to meet all Run-in Period completion criteria and were
not assigned to treatment. A total of 38 subjects with Type I or Type II HAE were randomized in
Treatment Period 1, and 6 additional subjects with FXII/PLG (Type III) HAE received 600 mg
garadacimab once every 4 weeks (open-label). All 38 subjects on blinded treatment completed
Treatment Period 1.
Overall, the subject disposition is generally reasonable for a phase 2 HAE trial. For further
information regarding the Type III HAE subjects, see Section 6.3.2.

Primary Efficacy Results
The primary efficacy endpoint was the time-normalized number of HAE attacks per month
during Treatment Period 1. This endpoint is consistent with the phase 3 trial (3001) and previous
programs for HAE prophylaxis. The calculation of the time-normalized number of HAE attacks
per month during Treatment Period 1 is provided in Section 15.3.3.
The primary efficacy results are shown in Table 117. The mean time-normalized number of HAE
attacks per month during Treatment Period 1 was lowest in the 200 mg garadacimab arm
(mean=0.05; 95% CI: -0.06, 0.15) compared to placebo (mean=4.24; 95% CI: 2.74, 5.75), with a
reduction in the mean of 98.9% relative to placebo. The mean time-normalized number of HAE
attacks per month during Treatment Period 1 was also considerably lower in the 600 mg
garadacimab arm (mean=0.35; 95% CI: -0.02, 0.73) compared to placebo (mean=4.24; 95% CI:
2.74, 5.75), with a reduction in the mean of 91.7% relative to placebo. These results signify an
improvement in both the 200 mg and 600 mg garadacimab arms compared to placebo. Further,
there was a statistically significant difference (Mann-Whitney p-value <0.001) in the rank sums
of the time-normalized number of HAE attacks per month in the 200 mg garadacimab and
placebo arms (i.e., the rank sum in the 200 mg garadacimab arm was significantly lower than the
placebo arm, assuming that a higher rank indicates a worse outcome). A similar conclusion can
be made for the 600 mg garadacimab arm versus placebo (Mann-Whitney p-value=0.001).
Finally, in an exploratory analysis, there was no statistical difference between the 200 mg and
600 mg garadacimab arms (Mann-Whitney p-value=0.085).

Secondary Efficacy Results
Table 118 shows the number and percentage of subjects with C1-INH (Type I or Type II) HAE
who had a ≥50% reduction in the time-normalized number of HAE attacks per month during
Treatment Period 1 compared to the Run-in Period. All garadacimab arms outperformed placebo
on this endpoint. All subjects in the 75 mg, 200 mg, and 400 mg garadacimab arms had a
reduction of at least 50%. Most subjects (86%) in the 600 mg garadacimab arm also met this
endpoint. No subjects on placebo achieved a ≥50% reduction.

The number and percentage of subjects with Type I or Type II HAE who were attack-free during

this endpoint. The majority of subjects in the 75 mg, 200 mg, and 400 mg garadacimab arms did
not experience any HAE attacks during Treatment Period 1. A total of 3/7 subjects (43%) in the
600 mg garadacimab arm were attack-free during Treatment Period 1. No subjects on placebo
met this endpoint.

The primary and secondary efficacy results reported above support the Applicant’s further

additional supportive evidence of the effectiveness of garadacimab in preventing HAE attacks.

16.2.1. Interim Analyses, Trial 2001
The first planned interim analysis was conducted after all C1-INH (Type I or Type II) HAE
subjects who received 75 mg, 200 mg, or 600 mg garadacimab every 4 weeks, or matched
placebo, completed Treatment Period 1. Confirmatory tests of 200 mg garadacimab versus
placebo and 600 mg garadacimab versus placebo were conducted. All efficacy results in
Section 16.2 and safety assessments are based on the first interim analysis.
The second planned interim analysis was conducted after all subjects with Type I or Type II
HAE assigned to receive 400 mg garadacimab Q2W and all subjects with FXII/PLG (Type III)
HAE who were assigned to receive 600 mg garadacimab every 4 weeks completed
Treatment Period 1. This interim analysis included subjects with all HAE subtypes (Type I,
Type II, and Type III). These data are not shown as all the results are open-label.
The interim analyses above were planned to provide information on safety, efficacy, and PK/PD
parameters for Type I, Type II, and Type III HAE.

17. Clinical Safety
17.1. Subgroup Analyses, Trial 3001
The review team conducted additional safety analyses by sex, age, race and geography using
system organ class and preferred terms to assess for potential safety imbalances. These post hoc
subgroup analyses conducted on a small population have limited interpretability. But overall, no
imbalance was identified.

22. Data Integrity–Related Consults (Office of
Scientific Investigations, Other
Inspections)
For this BLA 761367, two clinical investigators, Drs Joshua Jacobs and Timothy Craig, and the
Applicant CSL Behring, LLC were inspected, covering two clinical studies: trial 3001 and
study 3002. Based on the inspections, the studies appeared to have been adequately conducted,
and the data generated by the clinical investigator sites and submitted by the Applicant appeared
acceptable in support of this BLA.
During the clinical investigator inspections, the source records related to the primary efficacy
endpoint and HAE attack characteristics (attack location, attack severity, on-demand therapies
used (if any) and trigger for attack) were reviewed and verified against the Applicant’s line
listings for the subjects. The inspections did not identify discrepancies related to the
documentation of the number of HAE attacks, which served as the basis for the primary efficacy
endpoint.
During Dr Jacob’s clinical site inspection, several discrepancies pertaining to the location of the
HAE attack and attack trigger were identified when comparing the source documents with the
Applicant’s line listings. Some of the discrepancies were due to a limitation within the case
report form and the electronic data capture system, which only allowed three entries per
anatomical location. As a result, some of the attack locations were grouped into general body
areas. See the Clinical Inspection Summary dated August 22, 2024, for additional details. The
discrepancies related to the attack location and trigger did not affect the primary efficacy
endpoint.

23. Labeling: Key Changes and Considerations
This PI review includes a high-level summary of the rationale for major changes to the finalized
PI as compared to the Applicant’s draft PI (Table 126). The PI was reviewed to ensure that PI
meets regulatory/statutory requirements, is consistent with labeling guidance, conveys clinically
meaningful and scientifically accurate information needed for the safe and effective use of the
drug, and provides clear and concise information for the healthcare practitioner.
Table 126. Key Labeling Changes and Considerations
Rationale for Major Changes to FDA Revised PI2 Compared
Full PI Sections1
to Applicant’s Draft PI
No changes – the indication statement is consistent with other
1 INDICATIONS AND USAGE
approved product labeling for HAE prophylactic therapies
The recommended dosage of ANDEMBRY is an initial loading
dose of 400 mg (two 200 mg injections) administered
subcutaneously on the first day of treatment followed by a
maintenance dose of 200 mg administered subcutaneously every
month.
2 DOSAGE AND ADMINISTRATION

added to Section 2 to provide healthcare providers with
instructions for the use of ANDEMBRY, which is intended for selfadministration by the patient and/or caregiver. Therefore, the
(b) (4) patients for subcutaneous injection

technique.

5 WARNINGS AND PRECAUTIONS

6 ADVERSE REACTIONS



.
The proposed label lacked information regarding the number
of adult and adolescent subjects who had been exposed to
garadacimab, specifically subjects who had served as the
basis for the safety evaluation. The review team added
language that the Clinical Trials Experience subsection
reflects the safety of ANDEMBRY from the exposure of 161
adult and adolescent patients aged 12 years and older who
received at least one dose of ANDEMBRY 200 mg
subcutaneously.
(b) (4) injection site
reactions, which occurred more frequently in the placebo arm
in trial 3001 (VANGUARD), as an adverse reaction. A revised
adverse drugs reaction table included TEAEs that occurred
with ≥7% frequency with garadacimab treatment and greater
than placebo.
A new subheading for prolonged coagulation lab parameters
was added to convey that abnormal aPTT and PT findings
were observed in trial 3001 but were not associated with
increased risk of bleeding, as this may be useful information
for providers. Given that the lab tests are not clinically
significant findings, the results were not added to the ADR
table, but as a separate lab test subheading. This information
was included in Section 6 rather than 12 because it was
observed too infrequently to be considered a PD finding.

Rationale for Major Changes to FDA Revised PI2 Compared
to Applicant’s Draft PI
A Drug-Laboratory test interaction is provided because
ANDEMBRY can prolong activated partial thromboplastin time
(aPTT). The reagents used in the aPTT laboratory test initiates
an intrinsic coagulation through the activation of FXII, inhibiting
plasma FXIIa by ANDEMBRY and can prolong aPTT in the
assay.

2 Drug Information
2.1 Drug
CAS Registry Number:
2162134-62-3
Generic Name
Garadacimab
Code Names:
CSL312
Chemical Name
Human IgG4 monoclonal antibody that binds to activated human coagulation factor XII
(FXIIa)
Molecular Formula/Molecular Weight
Human monoclonal Ab/ 148.2 kDa
Structure or Biochemical Description
CSL312 is a fully human IgG4 monoclonal antibody comprised of two heavy and two
light chains, joined by disulfide bonds. CSL312 binds to activated human coagulation
factor XII (FXIIa) and inhibits its catalytic activity.
